# Ralph Progress Log

## Codebase Patterns

- PowerShell scripts: ASCII only, Set-StrictMode -Version Latest, read text with Read-Utf8Text (explicit UTF-8), avoid New/Set/Remove/Start/Stop/Update verbs on private helpers unless SupportsShouldProcess is added, use $script: variables across Pester BeforeAll/It blocks to keep PSScriptAnalyzer clean.
- Never redirect native stderr (2>&1) while $ErrorActionPreference is Stop in Windows PowerShell 5.1; lower it locally first.
- Functions that return an exit code are cast with [int] by the caller, so stray pipeline output fails loudly instead of being read as a pass.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1: never assign arrays through an if expression ($x = if (...) { @() } else { @('a') }); an empty array becomes $null and a one-item array becomes a scalar, which breaks .Length under StrictMode. Assign a default, then overwrite in an if statement.
- A PowerShell entry point that forwards options to another tool must not declare parameters; read $args so tokens like -k or -v are not bound as PowerShell parameters.
- Native command output inside a PowerShell function that returns a status must be discarded ($null = & docker build -q ...) or sent to Out-Host, or it becomes part of the return value.
- Logging safety is enforced by an allowlist processor that runs last, not by a denylist: free text only in the constant `event`, identifiers as short tokens, numeric measures by suffix, exceptions without messages.
- Verify a capability by reading the effective state back (the journal mode SQLite reports, a probe table) instead of trusting that a setting was accepted.
- Report optional dependencies, such as the control API, in a separate field of the readiness report so they cannot turn the service not-ready.
- Keep each refusal reachable in a unit test through scripted capability answers, and also prove the real behaviour in an integration test on real files.
- Each Python component has <name>/Dockerfile with lock, lockfile, deps and test stages, and its own pyproject.toml with the same ruff, mypy and pytest settings. Add the name to $script:Components in scripts/lib/PythonComponents.psm1, and add a `!<name>/tests` exception to .dockerignore.
- Tests for a documented table restate the documented data in the test, so the test fails if the code and the docs drift apart. They then check every ordered pair, not only the legal edges.
- When ruff's naming rules conflict with a name fixed by the docs, use a targeted `# noqa: <code>` and put the reason on the line above.
- Prove that an allowlist is closed with three tests together: enumerate every request the single builder can produce and compare it with a set written out again in the test; show that the builder refuses everything else before a request exists; and use AST checks to show that the module has no other path to the network (pinned imports, one client, no shortcut functions).
- Structural source scans should flag the dangerous construct itself (for example the docker command alone or followed by a subcommand), not every message that names it, so ordinary error text does not trip them. Include negative cases for such messages.
- When two Python packages must enforce the same rule but share no code, copy the scanner into each test suite with a 'change both together' comment that names the other file, and make each copy assert that the folder it scans holds modules, so an empty scan cannot pass.
- Put authentication in middleware before routing, not in per-route dependencies. Then routes that don't exist and routes added later are covered too, and a 401 never reveals which routes exist.
- When a direct dependency drifts a shared transitive package away from the version in docs/technology.md, pin it with `[tool.uv] constraint-dependencies` instead of adding it as a dependency.
- Test constant-time token comparison by replacing `hmac.compare_digest` with a spy through the `hmac` module, and assert that every refusal went through it.
- Prove a lint rule with fixture files kept in the config's `ignores`. A `node --test` file lints them through the ESLint API with `ignore: false`, using the project's real config, and asserts the exact reported lines.
- In nginx, put security headers once at server level with `always`, and set caching with `expires` instead of `add_header`. A location's own `add_header` would stop it inheriting the server-level headers.
- Write tool scripts as ES modules that export pure functions for unit tests and run their CLI part only when `import.meta.url` equals `pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href`.
- A generated artifact that is committed gets two gates: a producer-side test comparing it with the live source, and a consumer-side lint that regenerates into a temporary folder and diffs it (line ends normalized).
- Browser APIs that tests must script, such as WebSocket, go behind an InjectionToken factory with a minimal structural interface. Tests then provide a fake and drive time with Vitest fake timers.
- Pass third-party generator configuration from code in a tool script shared by the generate and check commands. Do not rely on a config file the tool discovers on its own.
- When a Dockerfile's last stage is a test stage, name the runtime stage explicitly with `target:` in Compose.
- Use `env_file` with `required: false` where a missing `.env` should be reported by the app's own start-up check.
- Ruff SIM113 flags a manual counter in a `for` loop; use `enumerate` and let the loop index carry the count.
- Keep docstring lines at 120 characters or fewer; ruff E501 covers docstrings in test fakes too.
- A test level that starts containers should clear any earlier project first, run in try/finally, always remove containers, networks and volumes, and then verify by label that nothing is left. A removal that cannot be verified counts as a failure.
- Name the Compose project with `-p` on every command of a test level, so an environment variable cannot point cleanup at a real installation.
- Invoke external tools by bare name (`docker`, `npx.cmd`) rather than `docker.exe` so Pester stand-ins first on PATH can intercept them.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 drops double quotes inside native arguments: use Go templates without quotes, such as {{json .Labels}} or {{.Names}}, never {{index .Labels "x"}} or {{.Label "x"}}.
- Wrap single-line native output in @() before indexing: a function returning @($output) with one line gives back a string, and [0] is its first character.
- Call %SystemRoot%\System32\tar.exe explicitly: from Git Bash, tar is GNU tar and reads C:\ paths as a remote host.
- PowerShell variable names ignore case: inside a Pester -ForEach case, a local $row overwrites the case data $Row.
- When capturing native stderr with 2>&1, set $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue' locally so PS 5.1 error records never throw under 'Stop'.
- Capture Write-Host output of a function in tests with `6>&1` and separate the returned [int] from the InformationRecords.
- Mandatory PowerShell parameters that take a collection which may be empty need [AllowEmptyCollection()].
- Keep .ps1/.psm1 files ASCII for Windows PowerShell 5.1: build non-ASCII output such as 'è' with [char]0x00E8.
- Lifecycle commands return an [int] exit status and print only with Write-Host or Out-Host, so lyrveil.ps1 can `exit ([int]$status)` and tests can capture text with 6>&1.
- Test helpers in tests/docker must avoid the verbs New/Remove/Set without ShouldProcess, because PSScriptAnalyzer runs on test files too.
- Code that runs in the only container with a route out imports the standard library only, enforced by a unit test that compares its imports to sys.stdlib_module_names.
- Docker test projects that need network fixtures share generated data (catalog, CA) through a project-owned volume and gate on it with a health check, so nothing reaches the Internet or an installation's volumes.
- Native tools whose exit status is part of the contract avoid argparse's exit 2 by overriding ArgumentParser.error, because 2 is reserved for preflight failures.
- One-off maintenance containers use Get-ToolContainerArgument in scripts/lib/DataFiles.psm1: docker run --rm --pull never --network none --read-only --tmpfs /tmp --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges:true --user 10001:10001, with --mount (not --volume) so Windows sources such as C:\ stay unambiguous; refuse folders with commas or double quotes.
- Write files that must appear whole under a temporary name, then hard-link (no-clobber) or rename into place, and verify the result (hash or check) before giving it its final name.
- Python code under backend/src must not contain the docker command text (the socket scan in test_docker_socket.py flags it); keep container invocations in the PowerShell scripts.
- In PowerShell 5.1 test code, avoid the verb New- for helpers (PSUseShouldProcessForStateChangingFunctions); Write-/Get-/Invoke-/Export- are safe.
- When nginx and the backend both set a response header, hide the upstream copy with proxy_hide_header: repeated COOP or CORP values stop parsing and browsers ignore them.
- Starlette exception handlers for Exception run outside the user middleware stack, so they must set security headers themselves.
- Log refusals with a constant event name and error_code only, never the refused Host or Origin value.
- Prove that a container has no way out without needing the Internet: check that /proc/net/route and /proc/net/ipv6_route (ignoring lo) have no default route, and use a container on a bridge network as the positive control.
- Pass Python one-liners to docker from Windows PowerShell with single quotes only inside the code, so the native argument is not mangled.
- Resolve compose.yaml with `--env-file <empty file in TestDrive>` and with the interpolated variables removed from the process environment, so a test judges the file's defaults and never reads a real .env.
- Each CI job runs the commands of the matching local level. A job that cannot call scripts\lyrveil.cmd (Windows-only) calls an exported function of the same module instead.
- Third-party release binaries fetched in CI are checked against the release's checksum file before use.
- Prove a capability in the real image with an exec case in the Pester lifecycle test that queries the app's own readiness route, not only with in-process tests.
- Run `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test <level>` piped to grep on the summary lines to get result counts without the full output.
- Declare expected-hash and expected-size build ARGs after the download RUN, so mismatch tests reuse the cached download and still run the check.
- For container tests from Windows PowerShell, pass file content as base64 to `bash -c` with a script that has no double quotes, and use tmpfs mounts in place of read-only volumes, so no bind mount is needed.
- Entrypoints that validate config can offer a --print-command mode that runs every check and prints the argv, so the command logic is testable with no GPU or model.
- Strict, frozen, extra=forbid Pydantic models for config files. `parse_catalog` turns the ValidationError into one CatalogError that lists every problem with its field location.
- A test that parses a documentation table and compares it with a shipped data file keeps the docs and the data from drifting. Copy the page into the test stage and use `.dockerignore` negations, as docs/runtime-contracts.md already does.
- Mutate shared asyncio state only from synchronous methods that never await. Before each await in a long operation, enter a state that refuses concurrent requests (LOADING or UNLOADING) so those requests see consistent state.
- Map port errors (gate, NVML) to the documented error codes at the adapter boundary (SupervisorError.code), so callers report `last_error` without parsing messages.
- Mypy strict flags a repeated `assert obj.prop is X` after a mutation as non-overlapping. Compare a tuple such as `(obj.state, obj.lease) == (...)` instead.
- Adapters list every path they call in a module-level ENDPOINTS constant, and a unit test parses the docs/runtime-contracts.md tables and fails on any '/api/' string constant in the module that the contract does not list.
- Bound streaming reads with anyio.fail_after around each `anext()` call, never around a `yield`, so one wait can tell a stall from the total deadline.
- Pure renderers that feed the KoboldCpp prompt cache return the full text plus the prefix that stays stable, so tests can check byte-for-byte prefix reuse without a model.
- Neutralize untrusted text at the single point where control markers are written, so only that code can produce real markers.
- Repositories take a caller-owned SQLAlchemy Connection and never commit, so domain changes and the jobs they create share one transaction.
- Store timestamps with lyrveil.domain.identifiers.to_utc_text (UTC, milliseconds, Z) so text comparison in SQL follows time order. Primary keys use new_id() (UUIDv7).
- ruff's N818 rule requires the Error suffix on exception names, and UP047 requires PEP 695 type parameters (def f[T](...)) instead of TypeVar.
- In tests, use a typed inner function with a Connection parameter instead of a lambda when calling write(engine, fn).
- Save first, then queue: commit the user's data in its own transaction and queue the job in a second one. A failure to queue marks the placeholder failed instead of rolling back the user's text, and a retry under the same idempotency key finishes the work.
- For any retryable action, derive the job's `idempotency_key` from a stable id (`chat_reply:<message id>`), and have the retry path check whether the job exists before resetting a failed placeholder.
- Services raise DomainError subclasses (NotFoundError, ConflictError, ValidationFailedError, PayloadTooLargeError). The API maps them to problem+json through the shared handler, so routers hold no error logic.
- A job handler that owns domain state settles it on failure itself, using the queue's retry rule (retryable and job.attempt < job.max_attempts), inside `anyio.CancelScope(shield=True)`, then re-raises so the scheduler stores the job outcome.
- Test WebSocket streams with raw ASGI messages on the test's own event loop and `anyio.fail_after`, not Starlette's blocking test session, so a missing frame fails instead of hanging.
- Keep one shared dict builder for public documents, such as `message_document`, and have REST models validate from it (`Model.model_validate(...)`), so REST and events never disagree on shape.
- Turn user text into FTS5 queries by extracting \w+ words and double-quoting each one. Never pass raw user text to MATCH.
- Keep FTS tables in step with their source rows through SQL triggers in the same transaction while no application-side transformation (such as stemming) is needed.
- A list screen keeps its state in a root-provided store of readonly signals. Actions call REST and then reload, and a reload overtaken by a newer one is dropped by a ticket counter.
- Open a modal with a native <dialog> element and showModal(), which gives Esc, focus trapping and focus return for free. Row menus are disclosure buttons (aria-expanded, plain buttons), not role=menu, so no arrow-key behavior is implied. Close them with a host binding on document:keydown.escape, because the template lint rejects key handlers on non-focusable elements.
- Catalog keys must be literal TextKey values. For dynamic labels, use typed option arrays or objects with `satisfies Record<..., TextKey>`, and render them with `option.label | t`.
- e2e: wait for a control that appears on both outcomes of an async step (for example the Annulla button) instead of waiting for a loading text to disappear, which passes before the loading text has rendered.
- Composition roots take adapter factories (`run(gate_factory, probe_factory)`), and the production `main()` passes only the real adapters. Test wiring lives in a tests/fakes entry point built into a test-only Dockerfile stage, never in a setting.
- tests/fakes modules are linted by both the backend and the orchestrator, which classify lyrveil_orchestrator differently (third-party vs first-party). A fake that imports both lyrveil_orchestrator and sibling fakes should import the siblings relatively, so the import order passes ruff in both components.
- Regenerate a component's lock with `docker build -f <component>/Dockerfile --target lockfile --output type=local,dest=<component> .`, then add the pinned version to that component's tests/unit/test_locked_environment.py.
- Keep index.html free of a <base> element, since the CSP says base-uri 'none'. Serve assets by absolute URL (deployUrl: "/") and provide APP_BASE_HREF, so a client route of any depth loads on a direct visit.
- Write e2e helpers that retry a click until its effect shows, using expect(async () => {...}).toPass(). Use this wherever a dialog or list can repaint under the click.
- Bind message text with interpolation, never innerHTML, and style it with white-space: pre-wrap. T-SEC-03 then holds without sanitizing.
- Record an exit as a dated docs/verification file: exact commands, versions printed by the commands, result counts per level, a test-to-file table, and an explicit Not run section. Link it under the phase's Evidence list and never edit an item line.
- Add a new table in its own Alembic revision, back-fill a default child row for existing parents in the same migration, and make the reader tolerate a missing child row.
- Keep a data file such as the tag vocabulary beside the code that uses it, with a loader that refuses malformed entries, so a packaging mistake fails in a unit test.
- A migration that must populate a new table from existing rows builds the data in SQL, using SQLite JSON functions such as json_object and json_group_array. It does not import application code, which will change after the migration is written.
- An append-only table gets a BEFORE UPDATE trigger that raises ABORT, plus a test that proves the update is refused. Foreign keys with RESTRICT already protect against deletes.
- Services compare the validated new value with the stored one inside the write transaction and skip the write when they are equal. That is how a 'save with no change' creates no version.
- Validation rules live as pure functions returning a catalog key plus params, mirroring the backend rules. The form schema, unit tests and any other caller share them.
- A FormValueControl for list-like values (tag-picker) is bound with `[formField]` inside a `lyr-field` shell that shows the field's first error once it is touched.
- Proposals that follow another field (subject tag from gender, Lei from a formal register) run in the control's `(change)` handler and stop once the user chooses by hand. They are not effects, since effects are reserved for external sync such as the local draft.
- A destructive action takes a server-side confirmation (the typed name in the body) and a preview of counts, and the client disables its button until the same text matches. The server stays authoritative.
- One row-level action runs at a time through a shared `run()` wrapper that sets `busy`, clears the previous notice and error, and turns a failure into an `ApiError` shown with the server's `detail`.
- A service operation that mutates several tables runs in one `write()` transaction, and the persistence function lists its delete order explicitly.
- Structured LLM output: Pydantic model → schema_to_gbnf grammar → generate_structured (validate, one repair, then StructuredOutputInvalidError) → JobFailedError(STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_INVALID, detail, retryable=False) in the handler.
- When a derived schema must mirror another (draft vs card), keep an explicit exclusion list and a unit test that compares the JSON Schema of every remaining field path, so drift fails a test.
- Routes that queue a job call `lyrveil.api.jobs.wake_worker(request)` after the commit; job details are read with `GET /jobs/{id}`, which never returns the payload.
- Do not guard tests with pytest.skip for platform features; tests run in the Linux backend container.
- Write files before their DB row and delete files after the row is gone, so a crash leaves at most an orphan file.
- Shared screen widgets that act on a stored entity take the entity id as a required input and reload from the server after each action.
- Show the catalog text of an ApiError code (`failure.textKey`) when the criterion demands Italian catalog messages, and do not fall back to `detail`.
- Declare fixed-path routes such as /import and /format-schema before /{character_id} so they are not read as ids.
- Read an upload with file.read(limit + 1) so an oversized file is refused without being loaded whole.
- Enforce a safety rule both in the shared validator and at the service's write funnel (_insert_new, _save), so restore, duplicate and future write paths cannot skip it.
- Give a safety refusal its own DomainError rather than subclassing the generic validation error, so callers that remap validation errors keep the safety code.
- Write long term lists as comma-separated strings in a constant (the formatter cannot reflow them) and unit-test that every entry equals its normalized form, so no entry is dead.
- Prove a safeguard is not configurable with an AST check of the module's imports and names plus an end-to-end test under non-default settings, hostile environment variables and settings rows.
- Keep test file basenames unique across tests/unit and tests/integration, or make both folders packages; mypy --strict refuses duplicate module names.
- Write paths that must flag a row (such as is_demo) pass the flag into the single transaction of _insert_new instead of updating afterwards.
- Copy-on-start: a conversation stores a JSON snapshot of anything it takes from a reusable template, and every later read (prompt, UI) uses the snapshot, so edits or deletion of the template never change a running story.
- Text that reaches the model from user-written templates (scenarios) goes through consistency.content_guard.find_violation and raises ContentPolicyBlockedError naming the field, never quoting the text.
- Do accent- and case-insensitive search of small tables in the service with a NFKD fold, because SQLite lower() is ASCII only.
- To tell whether SQLite is idle across processes, keep one read-only sqlite3 connection open and compare PRAGMA data_version between checks: it changes only when another connection commits.
- To pass Python code with double quotes into a container from Windows PowerShell 5.1, pipe it on stdin (`$code | & docker ... exec -T <service> python - <args>`) instead of passing it as a native argument, which drops the quotes.
- When two PowerShell modules would import each other, move the shared functions into a third module that both import; load it with -Force in the first one and without -Force everywhere else so one instance is reused.
- An evidence file lists the last run of each level, the failures of earlier runs with their fix, and what was not run, so nothing unrun is recorded as passed.
- A docker-level test that depends on image contents builds the image itself instead of trusting a tag that setup left from an earlier checkout.
- Keep module docstrings and test docstrings under 120 characters; ruff E501 applies to them.
- Derived search text (stems) is written by application code in the same transaction as the source row, through a persistence helper, not by SQL triggers.
- Quote every stem and join with OR before MATCH; stems are letters and digits only, so user text never reaches FTS query syntax.
- A job that stores model output writes its run row in a short transaction before generation. The commit transaction then reads the inputs again (cursor, input hash) and marks the run superseded instead of writing when they changed, so slow generation never commits against stale data.
- Model output that cites inputs by label (E1, P1, message seq) is resolved against maps built when the prompt was made; an unknown label drops only that item and increments a counter, and never refuses the whole output.
- Integration tests of model-backed jobs can use a scripted in-process TextRuntime (generate returns queued texts and records each TextRequest) with the real JobScheduler and a fake clock shared with JobQueue to step through backoffs.
- Keep model-output policy as a pure decision function (inputs: resolved item plus the existing rows it may touch; output: a frozen Decision) and let a thin service read the inputs and write the decision in the caller's transaction; unit-test the rules without a database.
- Every automated change to a memory row goes through a revise-then-update helper that snapshots the whole row into memory_revisions under its current version and bumps version, in the same transaction.
- Phrase lists per language live in prompts/<code>/<name>.txt and load with lyrveil.memory.window.phrase_patterns(language, file_name); match with is_significant.
- Write the tombstone before deleting the row, in the same transaction, so no deletion leaves nothing behind.
- Keep the pure decision (`is_blocked`) separate from the SQL that reads the tombstones, so unit tests cover the rule and integration tests cover the wiring.
- With SQLAlchemy text() and an IN list, keep the SQL in a module constant and write `text(SQL).bindparams(bindparam('name', expanding=True))`. This also avoids ruff-format ambiguity around chained calls.
- For scope isolation, check the conversation's own opt-in in SQL (EXISTS on conversations.shares_memory) as well as the caller's flag, so a wrong flag alone cannot leak data.
- When a SQL string would land near 120 columns, run lint: ruff format may join implicit string concatenations that fit.
- Make an append-only record immutable with a SQLite `BEFORE UPDATE OF <all columns except status>` trigger that raises ABORT, so a later version needs a new row.
- In a background job, re-read what the job covered inside the commit transaction and compare its hash. If the data changed during generation, write nothing, report `superseded` and re-check the trigger.
- Keep selection, chunking and rollup rules in a pure `memory/*.py` module that takes plain dataclasses, and keep the SQL in `persistence/*.py`. The rules can then be unit-tested without a database.
- Price per-family template overhead with a fixed probe: count two constant prompts that differ by known turns. The counter cache keeps the result per model, so it costs nothing after the first reply.
- Log fields must end in `_tokens` or `_count`, or be allowlisted identifiers. Log booleans as `<name>_count=int(flag)`; free text and string lists are dropped by `keep_allowed_fields`.
- To make a budget test deterministic, derive the squeezed window from a first build's `token_total`, and don't guess window sizes.
- Compute derived state only as a pure fold over the history rows, and rewrite the stored row from a fold of the rows read back after every change. Then the stored value equals a replay of the history by construction, and invalidating a row needs no special case.
- Rows from one run share their `created_at`; order such rows by `created_at, rowid`, because UUIDv7 ids made in the same millisecond have no defined order.
- Inside test loops, Ruff B023 flags lambdas that capture loop variables even when they are called at once. Move the call into a module-level helper that takes the values as parameters.
- Run derived-data invalidation in the caller's write transaction of the user change, and queue only the model work, so the change and its invalidation commit together or not at all.
- When re-reading old input, guard against double counting and overriding newer state by checking what applied rows already cite and whether newer rows exist, instead of trusting the model.
- ruff format joins a signature or call onto one line when it fits in exactly 120 characters; count before wrapping, or let `test lint` report the diff.
- Write streaming text transforms as a chain of character-level stages whose decisions depend only on the characters seen so far; the output is then independent of chunking by construction, and a test can feed every split position.
- Write look-alike or invisible characters in Python source with \N{NAME} escapes, both for readability and to satisfy RUF001.
- For text matched with regexes but reported at positions in the original, fold one character into one character (NFKD(c.lower())[:1]). Indices then stay aligned between the folded and the original or masked text.
- Streaming filters stay chunk-independent when they process one character at a time, or hold back only the longest suffix that is a proper prefix of a marker. Test this by splitting fixed inputs at every chunk size.
- Derive per-chat rolling state (like the adaptive notes) from what is already stored on earlier rows, not from in-memory dicts. It then survives restarts, and regenerating a reply reproduces the same inputs.
- Studio-style read services return plain dicts shaped for a pydantic response model; the router wraps them with Model(**dict), and each read names its owner (a character or a conversation) so data never mixes across chats.
- Frontend feature stores use a ticket per read (stale responses are dropped), keep the previous rows on a failed read and set a part-level error, and run every action as act(): call the API, read the list again, refresh any open detail.
- For repeated controls in Angular templates (cards), put `data-testid` on the card and scope e2e locators and unit queries to it; the unit specs use the fixture.whenStable + setTimeout `settle()` loop before and after flushing requests.
- To test a flow that spans the reply handler and the API, reuse `FakeRuntime`, `new_chat`, `worker_for` and `Ticks` from `integration.test_reply_streaming`, send with `MessageService(engine).send`, and run the job with `scheduler.run_once()`. A `TestClient` without a context manager does not run the app's worker.
- Anything written as a side effect of a reply goes in its own short transaction after the reply is stored, with a try/except that logs and continues, so it can never turn the reply into a failed job.
- Show stored memory text by reading it by id when it is displayed (nothing is copied into the diagnostic row), and say so in the UI when the row is gone.
- Record exit evidence in docs/verification/<date>-<phase>-exit.md with versions printed by the commands, exact commands, last-run counts, a test-to-file table, what failed and changed, and a Not run section; link it under an Evidence: list in the phase section without editing item lines.
- Runtime entrypoints read launch files as data: KEY=VALUE lines checked against anchored regexes, exit 78 naming the key but never the value, exit 64 for bad arguments, and --print-command for tests that need no GPU.
- Python runtime images: a lock stage with `uv pip compile --generate-hashes --only-binary :all:`, exported with a scratch `lockfile` stage, then `uv pip sync --require-hashes`, plus an offline `--no-index` install of the upstream requirements to catch a stale lock.
- Pester docker tests pass shell and Python scripts to containers in base64 arguments, and keep scripts free of double quotes, so Windows PowerShell 5.1 passes them unchanged.
- Runtime adapters list every endpoint in an ENDPOINTS frozenset. Their unit test reads the matching section of docs/runtime-contracts.md and AST-scans the module for path-like string constants that are not in the table.
- Use a TypeGuard[int] helper that excludes bool when narrowing JSON integers under mypy strict.
- Fill workflow templates by node id and check each node's class_type before setting an input. Prompt text goes in as data only, so it cannot change the graph.
- Keep one file per template version, registered in a table. A test compares the folder with the table and loads every file.
- Values derived from a pinned upstream tool (token counts, hashes) are produced by a build-only Dockerfile stage that writes the file via --output type=local, paired with a check stage run in the docker tests with --output type=cacheonly.
- Data-driven GBNF: build the schema from the Pydantic model, replace string sub-schemas with {'enum': [...]} from the data file in file order, and convert with json_schema_to_gbnf; validation still re-checks membership in code.
- Filters whose step order matters return their removals with a reason enum naming the step, so tests can assert the order without reaching into private helpers.
- Write long fixed tag lists as one comma-separated string split by a helper, so ruff format does not explode them one item per line.
- When a structured-task bound must match a Pydantic model, read it from Model.model_json_schema()['properties'][name]['maxItems'] rather than hard-coding it, so the grammar, the fallback and the model cannot drift.
- Only allowlisted log fields survive (lyrveil.logs): use error_code, *_count and the ids. A custom key such as `check` is dropped silently.
- To narrow a wide Literal to a narrower one under mypy strict, look the value up in a dict keyed by the narrow type instead of returning it after an == check.
- Tests for a multi-stage job drive the real JobScheduler with a mutable GPU mode (`lambda: harness.mode`), switching it between run_once calls to match each stage's required_mode.
- Make persistence idempotent by deriving ids and file names from the job id (uuid5) and keeping a unique job_id column; a repeat then overwrites the same files and inserts nothing.
- Event publishing must happen on the event loop: do the database work in a thread, return what to announce, and publish after the thread returns.
- Keep fixed word lists for deterministic detectors in prompts/<lang>/*.txt files, not in code, so a language without lists never matches.
- Document a backend-only change in the changelog and say plainly what is not delivered, so the entry stays honest.
- Keep form limits and request-body conversion in a pure module (image-request.ts) with a spec, and make the dialog a thin component over it.
- A dialog that sends through a store should get the failure back as a return value (`Promise<ApiError | null>`) and show it inside the dialog, since the screen's `actionError` banner sits behind the modal.
- To follow a queued job from a component, poll `GET /jobs/{id}` with a generation ticket and a stored timer handle, and clear both in `onDestroy` and when the input changes, so a stale poll never writes.
- Page lists by an opaque base64 cursor of (created_at, id) ordered descending, and fetch limit+1 rows to know whether another page exists.
- Keep the grid on thumbnail URLs only, with loading=lazy and content-visibility: auto on cells; open the original only in the lightbox.
- To test exclusivity, wrap `FakeContainerGate` so that each start checks that the other container is not running and the card's memory is released, and records any violation. Tie it to the `FakeGpuProbe`, setting `runtime_mib` on start and stop, so release drops can be checked against the footprint measured at load.
- Calls to the control API block, so the scheduler runs them via `anyio.to_thread.run_sync`. Never call them on the event loop.
- A test or spec that has never run should be treated as unverified. Its first run in an exit task often exposes faults in the test itself, and those are fixed in the test, not in product code.
- Record failed first runs in the evidence file together with the fix. List the last passing run in the results table.
- To prove a property test has teeth, temporarily disable the guard it protects, confirm Hypothesis finds a counterexample, then restore the code and confirm with git status that the file is unchanged.
- For step-by-step lifecycle tests, call the control API with httpx.ASGITransport (it runs no lifespan, so no run loop) and call manager.step() on the same event loop. This lets each documented state be checked between steps.
- Model the run loop in lifecycle property tests: let the manager step until it rests after each event, and inject 'during' events from the fake gate's calls, so requests and faults also arrive mid-step. Check invariants that only hold between steps (such as state-runtime agreement) only there.
- A supervisor under test needs its own clock that only its fake sleep advances; with a frozen clock the release-settle loop never times out.
- To end a queued job outside its stage, mark it running without a lease (JobQueue.start with lease_id None), call the handler's AbandoningHandler.abandon if it has one, then store the failure through the scheduler's normal _store path. Never fail a message-linked job in the queue alone.
- A condition the orchestrator reports without a state transition must still bump seq (an event with source == target), or long-poll followers keyed on seq will not see it.
- Memory checks that may block a runtime start must first rule out a running Lyrveil runtime container, or memory of a runtime started by hand is mistaken for another program's and recovery never runs.
- Keep scheduling decisions as pure functions over a snapshot (jobs/selection.py), and keep clocks and I/O in the scheduler, so the rules can be unit tested with plain values.
- To stop a running handler on purpose, set a reason on the run context before cancelling its scope. The handler's cancellation branch can then tell user cancellation and preemption apart from a lost lease or shutdown.
- When a route changes state the UI shows, return the events from the service and publish them after the commit with anyio.from_thread.run_sync(hub.publish, ...) from the route thread.
- In Playwright specs, hold the backend's WebSocket events and release them later in their original order, keeping their ids, instead of injecting frames with fake ids that make the client drop later real frames.
- Cross-service failure signals the backend must act on go into the long-polled state document as a record keyed by the event seq that persists until the condition clears, so a folded long poll cannot miss it; the consumer acts once per (boot_id, seq).
- When the backend answers an orchestrator event by changing a file, record the answered event in the file itself (a comment line) so a backend restart stays idempotent.
- Rewrite runtime launch files as KEY=VALUE data: change only the needed keys, keep other lines and the old file's permissions, write beside it and os.replace in one step.
- When a CLI process in the backend container must change the behaviour of the running server, pass the signal through a database row, and check it inside the same SQL statement that acts on it, so the two processes cannot race.
- Cleanup on cancellation (releasing a lease, aborting a runtime) belongs in 'except anyio.get_cancelled_exc_class()' with a shielded CancelScope. Catch and log any Exception inside it so nothing replaces the cancellation, then re-raise.
- Several observers behind one callback should be joined with a combinator that isolates each one's exceptions (lifecycle_events.each), so one failure does not stop the others.
- Work that must not overlap a GPU lease, such as a mode change, can run as a queued job whose stage needs no GPU (mode none). The single worker then serializes it with every other job, so nothing else asks for a mode while it runs.
- Accept a file name, never a path: check a strict name pattern, lstat to refuse symlinks, then compare the resolved parent with the root's resolved path joined with the folder name. Check again in the background job before reading the file.
- Do not use test.skip for a machine-dependent case in e2e. Make the state deterministic by rewriting the API response with page.route/route.fetch, or by seeding it.
- In Playwright helpers, for a dialog that renders in stages, wrap check-then-click in `expect(async () => {...}).toPass()` and finish on the element the next step needs.
- When a count in a docker-level test depends on files the app writes at startup, name those files in a comment next to the expected count.
- Pure rules take the time and the counted activity as arguments, so quiet hours and caps are unit-tested with fixed datetimes. The SQL lives in a store module that never commits.
- A package that must not cause a GPU mode change is guarded by an AST test over its imports, plus a check that the job it queues needs only the text mode.
- To reuse the reply pipeline for non-reply text, build a ContextRequest with no current message and an instruction; then stream through TurnCutoff, ReplyNormalizer, SentenceSplitter and `check_reply`, as `ProactiveMessageService._stream` and `ImagePromptStage.write_caption` do.
- A handler whose job moves an event or placeholder must also end it when the job fails for good: re-raise while the scheduler will retry (retryable and `attempt < max_attempts`, or preemption), otherwise end the event first. Also implement `abandon` and `cancelled`.
- Roll back a multi-row write on a late conflict by raising a private exception inside the `write()` closure, then catch it outside and decide what to end.
- A startup-only sweep belongs in the long-running service, before its first loop pass, wrapped so a failure is logged and never stops the loop.
- A step that keeps an item for later should move the item out of its own selection criterion, here by setting due_at to now, so running the step again finds nothing and adds no duplicate notification.
- A service that needs to announce something after its transaction returns a plain result (an id or a document) and the caller publishes on the event loop. This keeps worker-thread code free of hub calls and keeps equality in existing tests stable (use `field(compare=False)` for the extra id).
- For browser flows that depend on a feature with no switch yet, an e2e spec can play the REST routes with `page.route` and inject event frames with `page.routeWebSocket` (ids far above the real ones). The spec should say so in its header, and the backend half should have its own integration test.
- Derive a state that must stay consistent after edits, deletes and regenerations (such as the simulated read state) in the read query instead of storing it.
- Keep store side effects that call REST in the screen, driven by an effect on a computed signal. Store specs that call HttpTestingController.verify() stay unaffected.

Only the documented ID fields (as short tokens) and numeric size or timing fields are kept. - **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The commit-message checker rejects the whole words llm, ai, assistant, gpt, claude, codex and gemini, plus 'generated by', case-insensitively and at hyphen boundaries. 'lyrveil-llm' and the Compose profile name 'ai' therefore fail it: say 'the two runtime containers' or 'the text runtime' instead.
  - Running the commit-message checker through python in the shell was denied in this session; check messages by hand against its regex in ~/.claude/skills/ajimu-conventions/scripts/check_commit_message.py.
  - `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test unit <paths>` runs a component only when it holds every named path. Name backend and orchestrator files in separate commands, or nothing runs and the level fails.
  - ContainerGate API: inspect(name) -> ContainerStatus(state, running, health, exit_code); start(name) and stop(name, grace_s 1..120) -> bool (False when Docker answers 304); top(name) -> ProcessTable (empty on 409); logs(name) -> str (last 200 lines, never to be stored or logged). Errors: ContainerNotAllowedError and EndpointNotAllowedError (raised before any request); ForeignContainerError, ContainerMissingError (404, maps to RUNTIME_CONTAINER_MISSING), DockerUnavailableError (maps to DOCKER_UNAVAILABLE) and DockerResponseError(.status_code), all ContainerCallError with .name and .operation. Messages are safe to log.
  - Any new orchestrator module that imports socket or a Docker SDK, passes `uds=`, names docker.sock, unix:// or DOCKER_HOST, or holds the docker command as a string fails test_socket_confinement.py. The backend has the same scan in tests/unit/test_docker_socket.py; change both copies together.
  - test_container_gate.py pins the gate's imports (collections.abc, dataclasses, enum, httpx, struct, types, typing), requires exactly one httpx.AsyncClient and one AsyncHTTPTransport, and allows only aclose, build_request and send on self._client. Widen these on purpose if the gate needs more.
  - Async orchestrator tests use @pytest.mark.anyio with a module-scoped anyio_backend fixture that returns 'asyncio'; pytest-asyncio is not installed. respx clears router.calls when its `with` block ends, so assert on calls inside the block.

## 2026-11-06T21:30:54Z - P01.4c
- Delivered P01.4c in the orchestrator. `gpu_monitor.py` defines the `GpuProbe` port, `NvmlProbe` (makes only the documented NVML calls on device 0, rounds to MiB, raises `GpuProbeError` for any NVML failure including a missing library, and retries init on the next read) and `GPUResourceMonitor`. `preflight.py` judges the four orchestrator rows at the thresholds in docs/operations.md: CUDA at least 13.0, total VRAM pass from 11000 MiB, warning from 10000, failure below that; free VRAM pass from 9000 MiB, otherwise a warning, with a waiting note below 6000 and never a failure. When NVML fails, all four rows fail. It runs as `python -m lyrveil_orchestrator.preflight [--json]` and exits 0 with only warnings, 1 when a row fails. `api.py` has `create_app(control_token, monitor)`, which refuses a token that is not 64 hex characters. Middleware runs before routing and requires a Bearer token on every path except `GET /v1/health`, compared with `hmac.compare_digest`. Any failure gets the same 401 problem response with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. `GET /v1/preflight` returns the report. OpenAPI and docs pages are off. Added FastAPI 0.162.0 and nvidia-ml-py 13.615.71, held Pydantic at 2.14.2 with `constraint-dependencies`, and regenerated uv.lock through the Dockerfile lockfile stage. Created orchestrator/tests/integration, which `test integration` picks up on its own. ## 2026-11-06T21:45:29Z - P01.5a
- I created `frontend/`: a standalone Angular 22.2.4 app (CLI and build 22.2.5, TypeScript 6.0.3) with zoneless change detection set explicitly. Its route table covers the 13 documented screens. `/` and unknown paths go to `/chats`. Each screen loads lazily as a placeholder that shows its title and says it is not available yet.

The design tokens are in `src/styles/tokens.scss`, and the reset, typography and global stylesheets build on them. All three font families are bundled locally from the `@fontsource` packages at 5.3.0.

The text catalog is `src/i18n/it.json`, imported directly into the code so it works offline. The `Translator` service derives the key types from the catalog, so a wrong key fails the build. It handles placeholders and picks plural forms with `Intl.PluralRules`, and a `t` pipe gives templates the same checked access.

Lint and build checks:
- `eslint.config.mjs` adds the T-SEC-09 rule. It reports `[innerHTML]`/`[outerHTML]` bindings in templates and host metadata, `innerHTML`/`outerHTML`/`insertAdjacentHTML` in code, and any `bypassSecurityTrust*` call. It also forbids inline component styles.
- `tools/check-styles.mjs` fails on any color literal outside the token file.
- `tools/build-strict.mjs` runs the production `ng build` and fails on any `WARNING` line, on any reference to another origin, or when a font the CSS names is missing from the output.

`scripts/lib/FrontendComponent.psm1` connects all this to the test command. When `frontend/package.json` exists, it runs `npm ci --ignore-scripts` if `node_modules` is missing or older than the lock. `test lint` then runs `npm run lint` and `npm run build`, and `test unit` runs `npm run test` (Vitest through `ng test`, then the Node tests in `tools/`). I also wrote the two-stage `Dockerfile` and the `nginx.conf` (security headers, `/healthz`, proxying, caching).

Checks I ran:
- `npm run lint`: exit 0.
- `npm run build`: exit 0, no warnings, no network references, 32 font files in `dist/lyrveil/browser/media`.
- `npm run test`: exit 0. Vitest ran 2 files and 7 tests (translator and route table); the Node tests ran 7, including the T-SEC-09 fixture test, which reports exactly 3 lines in the HTML fixture and 6 in the TypeScript fixture.
- `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test lint`, run again after the doc edits: passed, with the frontend part included.

Not verified:
- The Docker image build and nginx at runtime (no frontend image build was allowed).
- A real build warning going through the gate; only the warning parser was tested.
- `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test unit` as a whole; I ran the frontend tests through npm directly.
- The app in a browser.
- Files changed: scripts/lib/TestCommand.psm1, scripts/lib/FrontendComponent.psm1, frontend/package.json, frontend/package-lock.json, frontend/angular.json, frontend/tsconfig.json, frontend/tsconfig.app.json, frontend/tsconfig.spec.json, frontend/eslint.config.mjs, frontend/Dockerfile, frontend/nginx.conf, frontend/src/index.html, frontend/src/main.ts, frontend/src/i18n/it.json, frontend/src/styles/tokens.scss, frontend/src/styles/reset.scss, frontend/src/styles/typography.scss, frontend/src/styles/global.scss, frontend/src/app/app.ts, frontend/src/app/app.html, frontend/src/app/app.scss, frontend/src/app/app.config.ts, frontend/src/app/app.routes.ts, frontend/src/app/app.routes.spec.ts, frontend/src/app/core/i18n/translator.ts, frontend/src/app/core/i18n/translate-pipe.ts, frontend/src/app/core/i18n/translator.spec.ts, frontend/src/app/core/shell/unavailable-screen.ts, frontend/src/app/core/shell/unavailable-screen.html, frontend/src/app/core/shell/unavailable-screen.scss, frontend/tools/build-strict.mjs, frontend/tools/check-styles.mjs, frontend/tools/tools.test.mjs, frontend/tools/eslint/unsafe-html.mjs, frontend/tools/eslint/unsafe-html.test.mjs, frontend/tools/eslint/fixtures/unsafe-html.html, frontend/tools/eslint/fixtures/unsafe-html.ts, CHANGELOG.md, docs/README.md, docs/frontend.md, docs/design-system.md, docs/testing.md, docs/technology.md, docs/verification/2026-11-06-frontend-shell.md
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Run frontend npm commands from the repo root with `--prefix frontend` (for example `npm run test --prefix frontend`). A bare `npm test` was denied; `npm run test` is allowed. A chained command such as `node --version; npm --version` was denied as a whole.
  - Never name an import `it` in spec files (for example `import it from '.../it.json'`): it shadows Vitest's global `it` and fails with TS2349. Name it `catalog` or `italian`.
  - Route data binds to component inputs through `withComponentInputBinding()`. Each placeholder route has `data.screen` (a TextKey). Delivering a real screen means replacing only `loadComponent` in `app.routes.ts`. The route spec checks the exact list of paths against docs/frontend.md.
  - Catalog keys are flat (`screen.chats`). An entry is either a string (a `TextKey`) or an object of plural forms with `other` required (a `PluralKey`). Add new interface text to `src/i18n/it.json`; the types update on their own.
  - Component prefixes are `lyr` for app components and `ob` for design-system primitives, set in angular.json and eslint.config.mjs. Component styles must go in .scss files referenced by `styleUrl`, because ESLint forbids inline `styles` and the color check reads only .scss and .css files.
  - `tools/build-strict.mjs` writes to `dist/lyrveil/browser`. It runs ng.js through `process.execPath`, without a shell, with NG_FORCE_TTY=false.
  - The package-lock was generated on Windows with `--ignore-scripts`. It already lists the Linux and musl packages (`@esbuild/linux-x64`, `@parcel/watcher-linux-x64-musl`), but `docker build -f frontend/Dockerfile .` has never run.
  - FrontendComponent.psm1 skips the frontend when `test unit` gets pytest arguments, and when `frontend/package.json` is absent (as in the Pester fixture repos).

I wrote `backend/openapi.json` by hand, because I couldn't run Python on the host. The test passed on the first run, so it matches. The new `openapi` Dockerfile stage regenerates the file after an API change, but I never built it.
- **Frontend drift check:** `tools/api-types.mjs check` is now the last step of `npm run lint`. It regenerates the types into a temporary folder and fails on any difference. The check reads the committed document and needs no Docker. `npm run api:generate` rewrites the committed types, and ESLint ignores that folder.
- **API layer:** `ApiClient`, `provideApi()` and `toApiError` are in `core/api/`. `provideApi()` is wired into `app.config.ts`. The catalog (`it.json`) now has 14 Italian error messages, one for each REST error code plus network and unknown errors.
- **WebSocket client:** `EventsSocket` reconnects after 1, 2, 5 and then every 10 seconds. It sends `hello` with the last event ID and `boot_id`, and it skips events it already delivered. It emits `resync$` when the server sends `resync.required`, when a reconnection has no event ID, or when `boot_id` changes. It also pings every 25 seconds.
- **Protocol assumption:** the docs don't say how the client learns `boot_id`, so I assumed the server sends a first `{"v":1,"type":"connected","boot_id":...}` frame on each connection. This is written into docs/api.md and flagged as an assumption for P02, which writes the server side. Nothing calls `connect()` yet.
- **Checks:**
  - `test lint` passes: ruff, `mypy --strict`, ESLint, the color check, the API types check, the strict build and the documentation checks.
  - Frontend tests pass: 28 Vitest tests and 11 Node tests.

  - Docs: I added a verification note (`docs/verification/2026-11-06-api-types-and-socket.md`) and updated api.md, backend.md, frontend.md, testing.md, the docs index and the changelog.
- Files changed: backend/openapi.json, backend/Dockerfile, backend/src/lyrveil/openapi_document.py, backend/tests/unit/test_openapi_document.py, frontend/package.json, frontend/package-lock.json, frontend/eslint.config.mjs, frontend/tools/api-types.mjs, frontend/tools/api-types.test.mjs, frontend/src/i18n/it.json, frontend/src/app/app.config.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/generated/index.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/generated/types.gen.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/api-error.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/api-error.spec.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/api-client.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/api-client.spec.ts, frontend/src/app/core/api/providers.ts, frontend/src/app/core/realtime/events-socket.ts, frontend/src/app/core/realtime/events-socket.spec.ts, CHANGELOG.md, docs/README.md, docs/api.md, docs/backend.md, docs/frontend.md, docs/testing.md, docs/verification/2026-11-06-api-types-and-socket.md

  - After any API change, run `docker build -f backend/Dockerfile --target openapi --output type=local,dest=backend .`, then `npm run api:generate --prefix frontend`, and commit both. The `openapi` Dockerfile stage was never built, so check it the first time it is used.
  - A targeted backend test runs with `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test unit tests/unit/<file>`. The path is relative to the component, not the repo root. The orchestrator reports 'skipped' for it.
  - The hey-api generator is driven by `frontend/tools/api-types.mjs`, which passes the config in code. There is no openapi-ts.config file, because the generator merges a config file over the arguments it is given. Only the `@hey-api/typescript` plugin runs, so `ApiClient` on HttpClient is the way to call the API.
  - The server side of /ws (P02) must send `{v:1,type:'connected',boot_id}` as its first frame on each connection, with no `id`. If it can't, change docs/api.md and `parseFrame` in events-socket.ts together. A `resync.required` frame may carry an `id`; the client uses it as its next `last_event_id`.
  - Nothing calls `EventsSocket.connect()` yet. The shell should do it when it shows the 'Lyrveil non risponde' notice, and a store should subscribe to `resync$` to reload through REST.
  - `SocketLike.onclose` is typed `CloseEvent` because a real WebSocket's `onclose` would not assign otherwise. A test double that fires close needs `new CloseEvent('close')`, which jsdom provides.
  - Keep the API error messages in `it.json` under `error.*`. `API_ERROR_TEXT` in api-error.ts maps each code to its key, and a spec checks that every key has a text.

## 2026-11-06T22:41:01Z - P01.6a
- Added setup.cmd, which only delegates to `scripts\lyrveil.ps1 setup %*`, and scripts/lib/Setup.psm1. Setup steps: (1) a six-row preflight (Git, Docker daemon, Compose >= 2.30, Engine API >= 1.45, memory seen by Docker, free disk on the drive of %LOCALAPPDATA%), printed as PASS/WARNING/FAILURE with advice; any failure exits 2 before anything changes. ## 2026-11-06T23:02:14Z - P01.6b
- Implemented start.cmd, stop.cmd, restart.cmd, status.cmd and logs.cmd. The logic is in the new scripts/lib/Lifecycle.psm1, called from scripts/lyrveil.ps1; Setup.psm1 now exports the helpers it reuses.

- **start**: runs the preflight rows except Git, and runs setup when .env, a volume, an image or a runtime container is missing. It then checks the volume labels and runs the orchestrator GPU preflight in a one-off `compose run` container. It starts only lyrveil-backend and lyrveil-frontend with `up --wait --wait-timeout 120`, and sends a GET to the published app port. It ends with `Lyrveil è pronto: <url>`, where the URL uses the port the Compose file publishes. Exit codes: 2 when a check fails, 3 with the last 50 log lines when health fails. The model files, the text model, the orchestrator and the backup copy print WARNING lines and never fail the start.
- **stop**: runs only `compose --profile ai stop --timeout 40`, never `down`. It exits 0 when already stopped or when Docker is not running.
- **restart**: stop, then start.
- **status / status -Json**: services, state and health, the URL and whether it answers, whether Lyrveil is ready, free disk, and null fields with warnings for what does not exist yet.
- **logs**: wraps `docker compose logs` with -Follow, -Since and -Tail.

The orchestrator is not started until P02. I followed the e2e level here, which also leaves it out, because `lyrveil_orchestrator.main` does not exist and its container exits at once.

Tests are in tests/docker/Lifecycle.Tests.ps1 and cover T-DCK-01 to T-DCK-05, status, -Json, logs, the no-Docker cases and argument refusal through each .cmd. They run against the project lyrveil-lifecycle-test, using compose.lifecycle-test.yaml. That override gives the project its own volumes and :lifecycle-test images, resets container_name and env_file, and publishes port 14210. A second override, compose.lifecycle-unhealthy.yaml, makes the backend's health check fail for the failure case. Docs updated: operations.md, testing.md, docs/README.md, CHANGELOG.md and a new verification note.

Verified: `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test docker` passed, 57 tests, 0 failed, in 103.29 s. The first run had 10 failures from two defects, both fixed. `test lint` passed.

## 2026-11-07T00:09:49Z - P01.6c
- `scripts\lyrveil.cmd models` is done and verified with a test catalog and a local HTTPS test server. On a real installation it still exits 1: `config/model-catalog.json` arrives only in P02.1a, so for now the command just reports that the catalog is missing.

**What was built:**
- **Download tool** (`backend/src/lyrveil/tools/models.py`): standard library only, run by the one-shot `lyrveil-models` service. That service is now in `compose.yaml` under the `tools` profile, on its own `lyrveil-download` network, and mounts only the models volume. It has five commands: plan, download, list, verify and remove.
- **Downloads:** HTTPS only, with certificate checks. Redirects are followed by the tool itself, only to HTTPS, and keep the Range header. An interrupted download resumes with a range request, with 5 retries 5 s apart. A wrong size or SHA-256 keeps nothing and exits 4. Any failure deletes the `.part` file, and files recorded in `/models/.verified.json` are skipped.
- **PowerShell command** (`scripts/lib/Models.psm1`): checks Docker, the labelled models volume and the image, and picks the tier from Docker's memory. It prints each license and asks once before any request (or takes `-AcceptModelLicenses`); with no answer or no console it exits 5. It handles `-List`, `-Verify` and `-Remove <id>`; `-Remove` refuses the default (active) profiles and asks first. `Read-CommandOption` gained repeatable options for `-Model`.

**Checks I ran:**
- `test lint` passed, before and after the doc changes.

- `test docker` passed: 70 tests, 0 failed. Before this task it had 57; the 13 new ones are T-DCK-11. I filtered the output, so I counted them rather than reading each line.

**Not run:** any real Internet download, the real `lyrveil` project, the out-of-space exit 2 path, and a typed "yes" at the console (tests mock the answer).

**Catalog format change:** docs/models.md did not say how a profile is marked as a tier's default. I added a `default_tiers` field to the spec and its examples, and the P02.1a catalog must use it.

## 2026-11-07T00:28:31Z - P01.6d
- Implemented `scripts\lyrveil.cmd import-model <file>`, `backup` and `restore`. Backend: new `lyrveil/tools/import_model.py`, standard library only. It refuses other extensions (case-sensitive), names outside `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}\.(gguf|safetensors)$` and existing names, and copies through a temporary name and a hard link, so no file is ever replaced. New `lyrveil/cli.py` has `backup`, `export-backups`, `restore-check` and `restore`. `backup` copies the database with the SQLite backup API and adds media/ (without media/.staging), runtime/ and a manifest.json with format, version, created_at, app_version, Alembic schema_revision and size and SHA-256 per file; the tar is checked before it gets its final name. `restore-check` refuses damaged archives, entries the manifest does not list, invalid paths, size or hash mismatches and unknown (newer) schema revisions. `restore` extracts to a staging folder with hashes checked again, then swaps db, media and runtime into place with renames, keeps the old ones in pre-restore-<time>/ and undoes the moves on failure. `persistence/migrations.py` gained `known_revisions()`. Windows side: new `scripts/lib/DataFiles.psm1`, wired into `scripts/lyrveil.ps1`. Every container is `docker run --network none --read-only --cap-drop ALL` as 10001:10001 from the local backend image. Import and restore mount the file's folder read-only; export mounts the backup folder writable and the data volume read-only. The backup folder comes from LYRVEIL_BACKUP_DIR in .env, with %VARIABLES% expanded, or %USERPROFILE%\Documents\Lyrveil\backups. `restore` runs restore-check first (exit 1, nothing stopped), then asks for confirmation (exit 5 with no answer), runs stop, then the restore container, then start, and returns start's status. Setup.psm1 now exports Get-EnvFileValue. The start/stop backup warning text no longer claims the backup command is missing. Checks: `test lint` passed after fixing one PSScriptAnalyzer and three mypy findings; `test unit -k "cli or import_model"` passed (43 backend tests including the socket scan, plus 3 matched orchestrator tests), before and after the fixes. NOT run: tests/scripts/DataFiles.Tests.ps1 (a Pester run outside the test command was denied) and tests/docker/Data.Tests.ps1. So the container-level behavior is unverified: export into a Windows bind mount as uid 10001, --mount with Windows sources, a backup while the backend runs, restore followed by start.
- Files changed: backend/src/lyrveil/cli.py, backend/src/lyrveil/tools/import_model.py, backend/src/lyrveil/persistence/migrations.py, backend/tests/unit/test_cli.py, backend/tests/unit/test_import_model_tool.py, scripts/lib/DataFiles.psm1, scripts/lib/Setup.psm1, scripts/lib/Lifecycle.psm1, scripts/lyrveil.ps1, tests/scripts/DataFiles.Tests.ps1, tests/docker/Data.Tests.ps1, tests/docker/compose.data-test.yaml, tests/docker/compose.lifecycle-test.yaml, docs/operations.md, docs/testing.md, docs/backend.md, docs/configuration.md, docs/README.md, docs/verification/2026-11-07-data-files.md, CHANGELOG.md

A backup needs an existing database with exactly one alembic_version row.

## 2026-11-07T00:38:37Z - P01.7a
- The backend now refuses requests that don't come from the local app. A request whose Host is not localhost or 127.0.0.1 on LYRVEIL_HTTP_PORT gets 403 FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN as application/problem+json. So does a write (any method other than GET or HEAD) or a /ws handshake that carries a foreign Origin. This works on REST and on the WebSocket handshake; the refusal body and the log never repeat the refused value. Both the backend and nginx.conf send the six documented security headers on every answer, errors included, and nginx removes the backend's copies so each header arrives only once. An unexpected exception now gives a generic 500 INTERNAL that carries the same headers. /ws now accepts the handshake and sends the `connected` frame with a per-process boot_id; events are left for P02.5c.

The allowed lists come from two new properties on Settings, both derived from LYRVEIL_HTTP_PORT: allowed_hosts and allowed_origins. With port 80 the names without a port are also allowed. The backend also accepts Host 127.0.0.1:8000, because the Compose health check connects that way. compose.test.yaml now gives the backend LYRVEIL_HTTP_PORT equal to the test port.

Checks: `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test lint` passed (docs, ruff, ruff format, mypy --strict, ESLint, strict build). `test unit` on the touched backend test files passed: the first run had 50 passing and 3 failing because of a mistake in my test, which I fixed. After the fix, test_local_only.py passed 27 of 27; the other touched files had already passed in the first run.

Not run: the e2e spec security-headers.spec.mjs (T-SEC-02 through nginx), the full integration level, and anything against the built images. So the nginx changes (proxy_hide_header) and the compose.test.yaml port setting have not been tested.

Known gap: the backend image has no WebSocket library. websockets 17.4 is listed in docs/technology.md but is in neither pyproject.toml nor uv.lock, so the real Uvicorn server cannot upgrade /ws yet. Only the in-process tests cover the handshake. docs/api.md says so.

Docs updated: security.md, api.md, backend.md, configuration.md, testing.md, frontend.md and CHANGELOG.md.
- Files changed: backend/src/lyrveil/api/security.py, backend/src/lyrveil/api/events.py, backend/src/lyrveil/main.py, backend/src/lyrveil/settings.py, backend/tests/integration/test_local_only.py, backend/tests/integration/test_health_and_startup.py, backend/tests/unit/test_app.py, backend/tests/unit/test_settings.py, frontend/nginx.conf, compose.test.yaml, tests/e2e/specs/security-headers.spec.mjs, docs/security.md, docs/api.md, docs/backend.md, docs/configuration.md, docs/testing.md, docs/frontend.md, CHANGELOG.md
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Every backend TestClient must use the app's address, e.g. TestClient(app, base_url="http://localhost:4200"); the default 'testserver' Host is now refused with 403.
  - Starlette's TestClient.websocket_connect resolves a relative path against ws://testserver and ignores base_url: pass a full URL such as ws://localhost:4200/ws.
  - A refused WebSocket handshake raises starlette.testclient.WebSocketDenialResponse (an httpx.Response) in tests; the middleware uses the websocket.http.response extension, or close 1008 when the server lacks it.
  - The backend image has no WebSocket library: websockets 17.4 (docs/technology.md) must be added to backend/pyproject.toml, uv.lock regenerated with the lockfile stage, and test_locked_environment.py updated before /ws works in the real server (P02.5c or earlier).
  - `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test unit <paths under tests/>` runs any backend test file, integration ones included; `test integration` takes no arguments.
  - tests/e2e/specs/security-headers.spec.mjs has not run yet; it expects nginx's CSP with port 4200 even on the test port 14200, each header exactly once, and FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN for a POST with a foreign Origin through nginx.
  - compose.test.yaml now sets LYRVEIL_HTTP_PORT to the test port; any other compose test file whose tests call /api through nginx must do the same, or the backend answers 403.

## 2026-11-07T01:45:35Z - P02.1
- Added docker/llm/Dockerfile (two stages on debian:trixie-slim pinned by digest). The download stage fetches koboldcpp-linux-x64 v1.124.2, stops the build unless the size is 628,981,944 bytes and the SHA-256 matches, runs --unpack /opt/koboldcpp, makes the result readable and runs koboldcpp-launcher --help. The runtime stage copies only /opt/koboldcpp, adds curl for the health check and the entrypoint, sets HOME=/tmp and USER 10001:10001. docker/llm/entrypoint.sh reads /run/lyrveil/llm-launch.env as data. It skips blanks and comments, requires each of the 11 keys exactly once with an allowed value and a readable model file, and otherwise exits 78 naming the key but not the value. --print-command prints the command without starting it, and any other argument exits 64. compose.yaml gains lyrveil-llm under the ai profile, as specified in docs/operations.md. Added tests/docker/TextRuntime.Tests.ps1, which covers the pin matching docs/technology.md, size and hash mismatch builds, image user and content, the launcher's --version and --help on a read-only root with only tmpfs /tmp, the mount table, a write to the root refused even as root, and the entrypoint's command and failure cases. Observed in this session: the real build passed (size and hash OK, unpack OK). The wrong-size and wrong-hash builds failed at the check step, reusing the cached download. Probe builds as user 10001 showed --version printing 1.124.2, every contract flag in --help, no model, .env or single-file binary in the image, and every entrypoint case behaving as intended. The image is 1.89 GB. Lint passed. Not observed: the new Pester file has not run, `docker compose --profile ai config --quiet` was denied by the session allowlist, and no model was loaded on a GPU. Setup builds the ai profile, so test overrides were adjusted. The setup override keeps the fake image but resets the inherited container_name, gpus, volumes and healthcheck. The lifecycle, data and models overrides move lyrveil-llm to a profile no command enables.
- Files changed: docker/llm/Dockerfile, docker/llm/entrypoint.sh, compose.yaml, tests/docker/TextRuntime.Tests.ps1, tests/docker/compose.setup-test.yaml, tests/docker/compose.lifecycle-test.yaml, tests/docker/compose.data-test.yaml, tests/docker/compose.models-test.yaml, scripts/lib/Setup.psm1, CHANGELOG.md, docs/operations.md, docs/configuration.md, docs/runtime-contracts.md, docs/testing.md, docs/security.md, docs/risks.md, docs/architecture.md

## 2026-11-07T02:07:41Z - P02.2
- The orchestrator can now start and stop the text runtime and hand out leases. The backend has a client that uses those leases. Lint passes and the new unit tests pass. The new integration test that covers the second acceptance criterion has not run yet.

What was built:
- **`supervisor.py`** (`ProcessSupervisor`): refuses to start a runtime while the other one runs (exclusivity rule 3). It starts `lyrveil-llm` through `ContainerGate` and polls Docker health until `healthy`, failing with `LLM_LOAD_FAILED`, or `RUNTIME_CONFIG_INVALID` when the runtime exits with status 78. On stop it uses the 20 s grace period, then checks `State.Running`, the exit code and `top`. It then samples NVML every 500 ms until three samples lie within 64 MiB, for at most 60 s. The VRAM growth at load and the drop at release are measured; they are recorded but never fail a check.
- **`lifecycle.py`** (`ModelLifecycleManager`): owns the state, the desired mode (default `llm`), the single lease, drain/resume, recover, the event buffer of 5000 and a reduced recovery. It applies one `state_machine.py` transition per step. Lease rules: one at a time (`LEASE_HELD`), mode match with no pending mode change or drain (`NOT_READY`), 30 s ttl renewed by heartbeat, and expiry moves to `RECOVERING` with `LEASE_LOST`. A release with `oom` or `runtime_error` goes to `RECOVERING`. A runtime that loses health ends the lease. A failed load is retried after 5 s and 20 s, and the third failure ends in `ERROR`.
- **`api.py`**: now serves every control route: state with long poll, desired-mode, leases (acquire, heartbeat, release), recover, drain, resume and events. The app's lifespan runs the manager loop. The signature is now `create_app(token, monitor, lifecycle)`, so the existing T-ORC-10 test was updated; its 404 check now targets an unknown path.
- **Backend `OrchestratorClient`** (`lyrveil/orchestration/client.py`): uses only the standard library and ignores proxy settings. `acquire_lease` raises `LeaseRefusedError` with `NOT_READY` or `LEASE_HELD`. `renew_lease` raises `LeaseLostError` on 410. `release_lease` returns False on 410. Anything else raises `OrchestratorUnavailableError`.

**Checks run:**
- `test lint`: passed for backend, orchestrator, frontend and docs.
- `test unit -k "test_leases or test_supervisor or test_orchestrator_client or test_socket_confinement"`: orchestrator 46 passed, including 11 T-ORC-03 tests and 10 supervisor tests; backend 10 passed.

**Not checked:**
- The `integration` level cannot be filtered, so `tests/integration/test_text_lifecycle.py` (start from mode `llm` → `LLM_READY` → lease → `POST /v1/drain` → `IDLE`) and the updated `test_control_api.py` have not run.
- The VRAM check before a load (`blocked_reason` is always null), the timed retry out of `ERROR`, launch-level fallbacks, log classification and the image flow are left for P06.
- There is still no `lyrveil_orchestrator.main`, so the real Compose service does not serve the API yet. Serving it needs Uvicorn added to the orchestrator lock.
- Files changed: orchestrator/src/lyrveil_orchestrator/supervisor.py, orchestrator/src/lyrveil_orchestrator/lifecycle.py, orchestrator/src/lyrveil_orchestrator/api.py, orchestrator/tests/integration/test_control_api.py, orchestrator/tests/integration/test_text_lifecycle.py, orchestrator/tests/unit/test_leases.py, orchestrator/tests/unit/test_supervisor.py, backend/src/lyrveil/orchestration/client.py, backend/tests/unit/test_orchestrator_client.py, docs/api.md, docs/backend.md, docs/gpu-orchestration.md, docs/testing.md
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `create_app(control_token, monitor, lifecycle)` now requires a ModelLifecycleManager, and its lifespan runs `lifecycle.run()`. Tests build one with `ModelLifecycleManager(ProcessSupervisor(FakeContainerGate(), monitor))`.
  - `lyrveil_orchestrator.main` still does not exist, and Uvicorn is not in the orchestrator's dependencies. Serving the API in Compose needs Uvicorn added to orchestrator/pyproject.toml and uv.lock regenerated through the Dockerfile lockfile stage.
  - `ModelLifecycleManager.step()` returns True when the state changed. Unit tests loop it until False (`settle`) and drive a fake monotonic clock; `ProcessSupervisor` accepts `sleep` and `clock` for the same purpose.
  - Lifecycle routes in api.py must be `async def`: the manager is not thread-safe, and a plain `def` route would run in FastAPI's thread pool.
  - The backend's `OrchestratorClient` is blocking (urllib with no proxy), so the P02.4 job queue must call it from a worker thread and heartbeat every `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_S` = 10.
  - A new operational code, GPU_RELEASE_UNVERIFIED, is documented in docs/api.md. The state document gained `draining`, and DELETE of a lease that is not current answers 410.
  - `scripts/lyrveil.cmd test integration` takes no arguments, so integration tests cannot be targeted. Only `test unit -k` can be.
  - Shell pipes to grep are denied, but `| head` and `| tail` on the test command work.
